really dark days, I catch myself thinking it would just be simpler if I didn’t wake up, and I’ve had these flashes of standing at the edge of the train platform on my commute and wondering what it feels like a personal attack on my genius. \n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I’ve been bouncing from rewriting project timelines at 2 a.m., to drafting long emails to executives, to reorganizing my whole apartment at dawn, barely sitting down or finishing anything because I feel this nonstop push to keep doing more. \n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): Some part of me has really believed that I’m the only person in the company who can see the “big picture,” like I’m operating on a different level than everyone else, and that if they’d just get out of my way I could single-handedly transform how the whole business runs. \n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): I’ve been sleeping maybe three hours a night, sometimes less, but I wake up—or just get up—feeling wired and convinced that sleep is a waste of time when my brain is “finally firing on all cylinders.” \n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): In meetings and even here talking to you, I feel like I can’t stop talking—I jump from one idea to the next, interrupt people, and steamroll over any silence because it’s physically uncomfortable not to get all the thoughts out. \n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind has been running like a browser with a hundred tabs functional_impairment: In the last few weeks my work has basically blown up—I was pulled from leading the rollout, HR got involved after complaints about my behavior in meetings, and outside of work I’ve been bailing on friends and letting basic stuff like laundry and bills slide because everything feels chaotic and unmanageable.\n- duration: All of this—the weeks of feeling super amped up with In the months leading up to the project’s launch, he pushed himself harder than usual—working late nights, stacking multiple side certifications on top of his day job, and treating his sleep, diet, and workouts as optimization variables rather than limits, all while brushing off his parents’ and sister’s concerns about his health. As the go-live date approached, his behavior at work became increasingly erratic: he abruptly overrode team input, made risky unilateral decisions outside established protocols, and began insisting on unconventional data-driven \"strategies\" that alarmed colleagues and led to complaints to HR when a client meeting went off the rails. The crisis point came when he was escorted from the office after showing up to a steering-committee meeting in an agitated and disorganized state; his manager and a close coworker convinced him to go to the emergency department for an urgent psychiatric evaluation after he refused to step back from his responsibilities despite obvious functional decline.\n- Chief Complaint: “For the last few weeks I’ve just been on this intense high, like I’m wired all the time and can’t turn it off, and it’s starting to scare people at work.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S020 (Hallucinations): (Depressed_Mood): When the buzz dies down, I crash into this heavy, gray feeling where I just sit on my couch staring at old sprint reports and thinking, “What’s the point, I’ve already ruined everything,” and it feels like that hopelessness is never going to lift. \n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): Even the stuff I used to genuinely enjoy, like weekend hikes or tinkering with side projects, feels flat and pointless now, so I just bail on plans with friends and let messages pile up because I can’t make myself care. \n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): For the past few weeks my eating has tasting it, and my clothes are already fitting looser. \n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either lie there wide awake replaying every meeting and imagining worst-case scenarios, or I jolt awake at three or four in the morning with my heart pounding and this dread that something terrible is about to happen with the rollout, and I can’t get back to sleep. \n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the really dark days, I catch myself thinking it would just be simpler if I didn’t wake up, and I’ve had these flashes of standing at the edge of the train platform on my commute and wondering what it would be like to just step forward, even though I haven’t actually made a plan to do it. \n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like I’m constantly supercharged and on edge at the same time—one minute I’m pacing around the conference room telling everyone this project is going to “change the industry,” and the next I’m snapping at my team for asking basic questions because any tiny delay feels like a personal attack on my genius. \n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I’ve been bouncing from rewriting project timelines at 2 a.m., to drafting long emails to executives, to reorganizing my whole apartment at dawn, barely sitting down or finishing anything because I feel this nonstop push to keep doing more. \n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): Some part of me has really believed that I’m the only person in the company who can see the “big picture,” like I’m operating on a different level than everyone else, and that if they’d just get out of my way I could single-handedly transform how the whole business runs. \n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): I’ve been sleeping maybe three hours a night, sometimes less, but I wake up—or just get up—feeling wired and convinced that sleep is a waste of time when my brain is “finally firing on all cylinders.” \n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): In meetings and even here talking to you, I feel like I can’t stop talking—I jump from one idea to the next, interrupt people, and steamroll over any silence because it’s physically uncomfortable not to get all the thoughts out. \n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind has been running like a browser with a hundred tabs open—while I’m trying to explain one strategy, I’m already three steps ahead thinking about new features, side businesses, and how this all ties into some bigger purpose, and I can’t slow it down enough to focus on just one thing.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations: The voice only started up once I hit this intense high around the project launch, and when I think back to earlier in my life or even during past low moods, I never heard anything like that or felt disconnected from reality unless I was in that revved-up, manic state.\n- functional_impairment: In the last few weeks my work has basically blown up—I was pulled from leading the rollout, HR got involved after complaints about my behavior in meetings, and outside of work None of this ever happened before; it only started once I hit this intense high with the project launch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a similar episode before, especially a period of feeling unusually high, needing very little sleep, or becoming overly energetic for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't recall ever having an episode quite like this before, at least nothing this extreme. I've had busy times at work in the past, but nothing where I felt this completely wired and out of control.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any earlier episodes of feeling unusually high, needing very little sleep, or being much more energetic than usual that lasted several days or longer, even if they were milder than this one?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having episodes of feeling that unusually high, energetic, or sleepless in the past. I have definitely experienced periods of really low mood before, but nothing like this revved-up feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of several days or longer when you felt very depressed, lost interest in things, or had low energy or hopelessness that might suggest a prior mood episode?\n\nPatient: Yes, for about three months now—actually starting just before this intense high hit—I've been stuck in this heavy, gray feeling of total hopelessness. I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like weekend hikes and my side projects, and I just feel like there's no point because I've already ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: How long has this intense high, decreased need for sleep, and feeling wired been going on continuously? Has it lasted at least one week?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's definitely been longer than a week. This intense high and feeling wired has been going on continuously for the last few weeks now. It just hasn't let up at all during this time.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice and beliefs only happen when your mood is very high or depressed, or do they ever continue when your mood feels normal?\n\nPatient: The voice and those intense beliefs only happen when I'm in that highly revved-up, manic state. I've never heard anything like that or felt disconnected from reality when my mood is normal, or even during my low periods.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice and strong beliefs ever continue for at least two weeks when your mood is not unusually high or depressed, or do they only happen during those mood episodes?"

No, they definitely don't continue when my mood is normal; they only happen during those intense high episodes. I have never heard that voice or felt disconnected from reality when my mood was stable or even during my low periods.

Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future. Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health. Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food). Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.
